**Corrections made during implementation** (this task's own review pass,
reading the plan sceptically per the warning at the top of this task, the
same way Tasks 1-9 each found defects review would otherwise have had to
catch):

1. The plan's `authenticate` snippet (Step 3) swallows two different kinds
   of failure into the same "not authenticated" outcome: a bearer token
   that resolves but whose `UserByID` lookup then errors, and a session
   cookie whose `UserForSession` call errors for a reason other than
   "no session" (`identity.ErrNoSession`). Both are silently discarded
   (the `if err == nil` / `if err == nil` shape only ever *sets* the
   caller on success and never inspects the failure), so a genuine
   database error at either of those two points would present to the
   client identically to an absent credential — a 401, not a 500 — which
   is exactly the ambiguity the task brief calls out as worth resolving
   deliberately ("what happens on a database error during authentication,
   as opposed to an absent or invalid credential"). The implementation
   splits `authenticate` into `resolveBearerCaller` and
   `resolveSessionCaller`, each returning `(Caller, bool, error)`: `bool`
   is the ordinary "found/not found" outcome (absent header, expired
   cookie, unknown or revoked token — all `(false, nil)`), and a non-nil
   `error` means the lookup itself failed and `authenticate` now answers
   with 500 for that case on both paths, matching what the plan's own
   snippet already did for `ResolveAPIToken`'s own error (the
   `errors.Is(err, identity.ErrTokenInvalid)` branch) but did not carry
   through to the two lookups after it.
2. `/healthz` stays public; `/version` does not — corrected in round 2
   below (Correction 6) after a spec review challenged this task's
   original reasoning that a public repository makes the commit SHA
   harmless. It doesn't: the risk is fingerprinting (matching a running
   instance's exact commit against whatever was patched after it), not
   the diff's confidentiality, and the public repository makes that
   matching easier, not moot.
3. The ticker for `PruneExpiredSessions`/`PruneExpiredInvites` is
   **not** wired here. `PruneExpiredInvites`'s own doc comment
   (`internal/identity/invites.go`) already names Task 16 as the owner
   ("See the plan's Task 16 note: this and `PruneExpiredSessions` both
   still want an owner"), and Task 16's own section below ("This task
   owns the expired-row sweep") independently confirms it. Task 10's file
   list never touches `cmd/maestro/main.go`, and `main.go` does not yet
   wire `Identity`/`Projects` into `web.NewServer` at all — there is no
   process-lifecycle home for a ticker to live in until Task 16 builds
   one. Wiring it here would either duplicate Task 16's work or misplace
   it in a package with no business owning a background scheduler.
4. Sliding session renewal (`identity.ExtendSession`) was originally left
   uncalled from `resolveSessionCaller`, matching the plan's own snippet,
   with the "renew at most once per interval" design decision punted to
   an unspecified future task. **Superseded in round 2 below (Correction
   7)** — punting it left `ExtendSession` with a test, no production
   caller, and a stale "Tasks 10/11" pointer in its own doc comment; round
   2 gives it a real owner instead.
5. `auth_test.go` covers more than the plan's three tests: a session
   cookie identifying its caller with a nil `ProjectID` (the plan tests
   only the bearer path end-to-end), an expired/revoked session cookie
   being unauthorized, an invalid bearer token *not* falling back to a
   valid cookie on the same request (pinning the precedence decision in
   Correction 1's neighbouring doc comment), and — the property this task
   exists to not reopen — a token whose creator has since been removed
   from the project being unauthorized, exercised through the full HTTP
   path rather than only at the `identity` package level where Task 9's
   own tests already cover it.

**Round 2 corrections** (from a spec review that attacked the boundary
directly — forged headers, a raw UUID in `Authorization`, query
parameters trying to inject `user_id`/`is_admin`/`project_id`, unusual
methods and encoded path shapes, a tampered token, an expelled member's
token driven through real HTTP, and a closed connection pool mid-request
to confirm a genuine infrastructure failure gives 500 while every bad
credential gives 401 — none of which got through, and Correction 1's
split of the error paths was confirmed to be a real fix rather than a
cosmetic one):

6. `/version` is now behind `requireCaller` (`/healthz` stays public).
   Round 1's reasoning (Correction 2 above) argued the repository being
   public made the commit SHA harmless; that argument answered the wrong
   question. The risk `/version` creates is fingerprinting: an
   unauthenticated caller can scan for the exact commit an instance is
   running and match it against whatever was patched afterward, at zero
   cost — the repository being public makes that matching *easier*, not
   moot, since it hands the attacker a precise diff of what the instance
   is missing. `/healthz` is treated differently on purpose, not folded
   into the same gate: liveness ("the process accepted this connection")
   carries no fingerprinting signal the way an exact commit does, and a
   load balancer's or orchestrator's probe needs to reach it without
   credentials. `handleVersion` now takes a `Caller` parameter like
   `handleMe` and is registered through `requireCaller`; any authenticated
   caller can read it, with no admin requirement.
7. Sliding session renewal is implemented, in `resolveSessionCaller`, not
   left for a later task — round 1's Correction 4 above is superseded.
   `ExtendSession` (Task 6) was drifting the way `VerifyDummy` did before
   it was deleted: a tested function with no caller and a doc comment
   pointing at two tasks ("Tasks 10/11") that had each individually
   declined to be the one that calls it. The middleware, not a Task 11
   login/logout/registration handler, is the right owner: resolving a
   session cookie into a `Caller` is the one thing every
   session-authenticated request does regardless of route, so it is the
   only place "this session is still in active use, worth renewing" can
   be decided — a login handler runs once per session and never sees the
   requests that follow it. On a session more than halfway through its
   `SESSION_TTL`, `resolveSessionCaller` now calls `ExtendSession` to push
   the expiry back out to a full `SESSION_TTL` from now; a failed extend
   is logged and the request proceeds with the caller it already
   resolved, the same non-fatal treatment `resolveBearerCaller` gives a
   failed token touch. The halfway threshold is what keeps this off the
   per-request write path — the exact hot-path mistake Task 9 fixed for
   token `last_used_at` via `touchThrottle` — not a per-request write with
   extra steps. `ExtendSession`'s doc comment (`internal/identity/sessions.go`)
   now names Task 10 alone, and Task 11's section below notes explicitly
   that it does not own this, so the ownership question cannot drift back
   to being unsettled the way it was before this round.
8. `auth_test.go` gained
   `TestVersionRejectsAnonymousRequests`,
   `TestVersionReturnsBuildVersionToAnAuthenticatedCaller`,
   `TestSessionRenewsPastHalfwayThroughItsLifetime` and
   `TestSessionDoesNotRenewBeforeHalfway` for Corrections 6 and 7;
   `server_test.go`'s `TestVersion` became
   `TestVersionRequiresAuthentication` (the unauthenticated 401 case,
   which needs no database) and the old `TestHealthzAndVersionStayPublic`
   split into `TestHealthzStaysPublic` and the new
   `TestVersionRejectsAnonymousRequests`.

**Round 3 corrections** (from a quality review that called the code
well-built and confirmed both round-2 deviations were improvements,
then found one live availability bug and several things that get harder
once six later tasks build on this package):

1. **Critical: a nil `Identity` panicked on `/healthz`.** `cmd/maestro/main.go`
   still built `web.NewServer(web.Options{Version: ...})` with no
   services, and `authenticate` now runs over the whole mux
   unconditionally. Any request to `/healthz` carrying a cookie
   dereferenced a nil `*identity.Service` inside `resolveSessionCaller` —
   `net/http` recovers per connection, so this dropped the connection
   rather than crashing the process, but it was reachable by an
   unauthenticated request against the endpoint an orchestrator polls
   for liveness. Round 1's Correction 3 argued `cmd/maestro/main.go` was
   out of scope for this task; that was true while `authenticate` was
   still a plan snippet and stopped being true the moment it became
   unconditional. Fixed on both ends: `NewServer` now panics at
   construction if `Identity` or `Projects` is nil — a misconfigured
   server refuses to start rather than serving everything except its
   first credentialed request — and `cmd/maestro/main.go` now opens a
   pool, runs migrations, and builds real `identity.Service`/
   `projects.Service` values before calling `NewServer`, which the new
   guard forces regardless. This is a minimal fix, not Task 16's full
   process lifecycle: no graceful shutdown, no first-admin bootstrap
   call, no prune ticker; `run()`'s own doc comment says so and points
   at Task 16 for the rest. `server_test.go`'s bare `Options{}`/
   `Options{Version: ...}` constructions became `stubOptions(version)`,
   building real-but-unconnected `identity.New(nil, config.Config{})`
   and `projects.New(nil)` values — enough to satisfy the guard for
   tests that never send a credential and so never actually query
   either service — plus two new tests,
   `TestNewServerPanicsWithoutIdentity` and
   `TestNewServerPanicsWithoutProjects`, pinning the guard directly.
2. **The sleeping renewal test is gone.** 120ms of sleep inside a 200ms
   TTL (Correction 7's original test) left roughly 80ms of margin for
   user creation, an HTTP round trip and two queries before an overshoot
   flipped the session from "past halfway" to "expired" — a hard 401,
   not a soft miss, under any load on the test machine. Rewritten to
   backdate `sessions.expires_at` directly with the pool, the same
   technique `TestExpiredSessionIsRejectedAndPruned` (Task 6) and
   `TestResolveAPITokenThrottlesLastUsedAtWrites` (Task 9) already use.
   An injectable fake clock was considered and rejected, on the
   reviewer's argument: expiry is enforced in SQL by
   `expires_at > now()`, so a Go-side fake clock would desynchronise
   from the database and the test would end up pinning a fiction instead
   of the real comparison `ExtendSession`/`UserForSession` make.
   `TestSessionRenewsPastHalfwayThroughItsLifetime` now brackets
   `now()+SessionTTL` around the request instead of asserting a loose
   "extended by at least N" — a renewal landed at the wrong offset (half
   the TTL, or the pre-cap value) fails this bracket where the old
   assertion would have passed it.
3. **`ExtendSession` gained a guard clause.** It had no equivalent to
   `TouchAPIToken`'s "this WHERE clause is what makes the write safe
   under concurrent resolves" — several tabs belonging to one session
   crossing halfway together all read the same `expires_at` and would
   all have issued an UPDATE against the same row, serialising on the
   lock for no purpose beyond the first writer's. **Superseded by Round
   4 below** — this correction's claim that requiring the new value to
   be strictly greater than the current one makes a concurrent loser
   "match zero rows — a no-op" was wrong: the new value was computed in
   Go, per request, so concurrent attempts do not share a target and
   each one's own, slightly later, timestamp still satisfies "greater
   than the now-committed row". A review proved this with `pg_sleep` and
   with fifteen real concurrent requests. This stays an UPDATE, never an
   upsert, which Round 4's fix does not change — that half of this
   correction was correct.
4. **Absolute session lifetime, decided: 90 days.** Sliding renewal on
   its own has no ceiling — a session in continuous use never expires,
   and a stolen cookie exercised even once a fortnight would be valid
   forever. `ExtendSession`'s query now caps the renewed value at
   `created_at + interval '90 days'` via `LEAST(...)`, applied on both
   sides of the Correction 3 guard so renewal simply stops (0 rows
   matched) once a session reaches the cap instead of drifting past it.
   `identity.maxSessionLifetime` (`sessions.go`) documents the same 90
   days for cross-reference; the SQL literal is what actually enforces
   it, the same division of labour `touchThrottle` and `TouchAPIToken`'s
   WHERE clause already establish for tokens. 90 days was chosen as long
   enough that a designer who opens their laptop weekly is never forced
   to log back in, and short enough that a credential nobody has used in
   three months stops being a standing risk on its own — written down
   here because the code previously made this decision silently, by not
   making it at all.
5. **`Caller`'s shape is now enforced by construction, not just
   documented.** With four exported fields and no constructor,
   `Caller{ProjectID: &id}` and `Caller{TokenID: &tid}` both compiled
   and would have been trusted downstream as though they were one of the
   two real shapes. `newTokenCaller` and `newSessionCaller` (unexported,
   `auth.go`) are now the only two places in this package that construct
   a `Caller` by literal; `resolveBearerCaller` and
   `resolveSessionCaller` both go through them instead of building one
   inline. `Caller` also gained `IsToken() bool` and
   `ScopedProject() (uuid.UUID, bool)` — the vocabulary Task 12 and
   every later task need instead of reading `TokenID`/`ProjectID`
   directly — and `ScopedProject` is where "an admin is not exempt from
   a token's binding" is now encoded exactly once (it never consults
   `IsAdmin`) instead of once per call site across six later tasks. The
   authorization companion this enables (`requireProjectMember` or
   equivalent) is explicitly **not** built here; Task 12's section now
   claims it by name, with the reasoning for why it belongs there
   (needs real endpoint shapes) rather than here.
6. **New tests for the admin invariant, precedence, and the 500 path.**
   No test before this round created an admin and asserted on
   `IsAdmin` — it was decoded and never checked, the exact untested
   invariant this task exists to protect.
   `TestAdminTokenReturnsProjectAndIsAdmin` bootstraps a real admin and
   asserts its token both reports `is_admin: true` and stays scoped to
   its own project, not exempted. `TestBearerTakesPrecedenceOverCookieForADifferentUser`
   sends a valid bearer for one user and a valid cookie for a different
   one — the case where precedence actually matters, since getting it
   wrong resolves the wrong identity for a valid request (a privilege
   issue) rather than merely producing a 401.
   `TestDatabaseErrorDuringBearerAuthenticationIsInternalError` and its
   session counterpart close the pool after minting a live credential
   and assert 500, pinning Correction 1's error-path split in the tree
   instead of leaving it proven once by hand against a broken pool.
   `TokenID` is now surfaced in `/api/me`'s `token_id` field
   (`handleMe`, `server.go`) rather than being set and never read;
   `TestBearerTokenIdentifiesCaller` asserts on it, and `handleMe`'s doc
   comment says plainly that nothing consumes it yet.
7. **Static assets and SSE get explicit forward notes, not code that
   doesn't exist yet.** No static file tree or SSE handler exists in the
   repository at this point in the plan, so nothing here short-circuits
   `authenticate` for a public prefix — there is no prefix yet to
   short-circuit for. Task 15's section now says explicitly that
   registering `GET /static/` (and `/login`, `/g/{game}`) must either
   sit outside the authenticated chain or make `authenticate` recognise
   and skip them, so a page with twenty embedded assets does not cost
   twenty identical session lookups. `authenticate`'s own doc comment
   (`auth.go`) now says authentication is evaluated once, at connection
   time — a long-lived SSE stream keeps running under the Caller it
   started with and never notices a logout, password change, token
   revocation or expulsion, and a session stream never slides forward
   after its handshake — and Task 14's section repeats this as a
   constraint that task must design around (most plausibly a bounded
   connection lifetime forcing periodic reconnection), not something
   Task 10 needed to, or could, fix by making `authenticate` re-run
   mid-stream.
8. **`GetLiveAPIToken` now joins `users` for `is_admin`.** This is the
   change on the `identity` side of the fold; see Task 9's own
   corrections for the trade-off it accepts (giving back part of the
   round-trip saving Task 9 fought for, to remove a different round
   trip here). `resolveBearerCaller` no longer calls `identity.UserByID`
   at all — `APITokenSummary.UserIsAdmin`, populated by the join, is
   enough — so the bearer path is one query plus, at most, one
   throttled `TouchAPIToken` write, not two queries plus that write.
9. **Minor fixes, all from the same review pass:** the wrapped
   `authenticate(s.mux)` handler is now built once in `NewServer` and
   stored on `Server`, not reallocated on every call to `ServeHTTP`;
   `authenticate`'s doc comment no longer claims any `Authorization`
   header takes the bearer path — only one naming the `Bearer` scheme
   does, everything else falls through to the cookie check;
   `writeJSON`'s own encode-failure body now reads
   `{"error":"internal_error","message":"failed to encode response"}`
   instead of a human sentence in the `error` field, matching every
   other error response's vocabulary; a 401 from `requireCaller` now
   sets `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer realm="maestro"`; every authenticated
   response gets `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Vary: Cookie,
   Authorization` (also added in `requireCaller`, so every current and
   future handler behind it gets both for free); and the two error
   codes this package writes (`unauthorized`, `internal_error`) are now
   named constants (`errCodeUnauthorized`, `errCodeInternal`) instead of
   being spelled inline at each call site.

**Round 4 correction** (from a re-review that verified almost everything
in Round 3 by attacking the running binary rather than reading the code —
confirmed `/healthz` with a cookie now answers 200, confirmed renewal
stops exactly at the 90-day cap by backdating `created_at` to 65 days,
confirmed the bearer path is one query via statement logging — and found
one real defect in the piece this round added):

10. **The Round 3 concurrency guard did not make concurrent renewals
    no-ops, and the comments claiming it did were wrong.** The target
    expiry was `time.Now().Add(ttl)`, computed in Go, once per request,
    in `auth.go`. Two requests crossing halfway within the same instant
    do not share that target — each computes its own, marginally later,
    wall-clock value, so a second writer blocking on the row lock wakes
    once the first commits, re-evaluates "is *my* target still greater
    than the committed row", finds yes, and writes again. A review
    proved this twice: holding the row lock open with `pg_sleep`
    produced two writes, and fifteen parallel requests against the real
    binary produced three, five and six writes across three trials — not
    one, ever. An earlier trial with more requests showed a single write
    and looked like success; that was a different bug wearing a
    disguise, not the guard working — later requests' own
    `UserForSession` had already observed the first write's renewed
    expiry and never attempted `ExtendSession` at all, so the "success"
    proved nothing about concurrent attempts that actually happen
    together. This is the third time in this project a comment promised
    more than its guard delivered (after the invite-sweep memory-bound
    claim and the `FOR UPDATE` lock test, both in Task 8's corrections);
    the fix below writes down what the code does, not what it was meant
    to do.

    Fixed by moving the target computation into the query itself and
    giving the predicate slack, per the reviewer's own proposed fix:

    ```sql
    UPDATE sessions
    SET expires_at = LEAST(created_at + interval '90 days', now() + sqlc.arg('ttl')::interval)
    WHERE token_hash = sqlc.arg('token_hash')::bytea
      AND expires_at < now() + sqlc.arg('ttl')::interval - interval '1 minute';
    ```

    `ExtendSession`'s SQL is now `:execrows`, not `:exec`, purely so a
    test can count actual writes instead of inferring them from the
    final `expires_at`, which looks identical whether one write happened
    or six (see below). `identity.Service.ExtendSession`'s Go signature
    changed from `(ctx, token, expiresAt time.Time) error` to `(ctx,
    token, ttl time.Duration) (int64, error)` — it hands Postgres the
    TTL and lets `now()` be Postgres' own, not the Go process', and
    returns rows affected (0 or 1, `token_hash` being the primary key)
    so a caller can observe whether its own call was the one that wrote.
    `resolveSessionCaller` (`auth.go`) passes `ttl` straight through and
    still discards the count — the Go-side halfway check still decides
    *whether* to attempt a renewal; only the SQL decides whether the
    write is still needed, exactly the division of labour the reviewer
    asked for. `TestExtendSessionPushesExpiryForward`
    (`internal/identity/sessions_test.go`) now backdates the session
    before calling `ExtendSession` (the one-minute slack would otherwise
    treat a session issued moments ago as already fresh enough to skip)
    and brackets the result around `now() + ttl` with a small
    clock-skew allowance, since the target is now computed by Postgres'
    clock, not the Go process'; the same allowance was added to
    `TestSessionRenewsPastHalfwayThroughItsLifetime`
    (`internal/web/auth_test.go`) for the same reason.

    The three overclaiming comments are corrected: `identity.sql`'s
    `ExtendSession` doc comment, `ExtendSession`'s Go doc comment
    (`sessions.go`), and `resolveSessionCaller`'s doc comment
    (`auth.go`) all now describe the one-minute-slack mechanism and say
    plainly that this does not collapse every N concurrent requests into
    one write in general — only ones that land within a minute of each
    other, which near-simultaneous requests do and requests genuinely
    spaced apart do not, the same throttle shape `touchThrottle` already
    gives `TouchAPIToken`.

    Pinned with `TestConcurrentRenewalsProduceExactlyOneWrite`
    (`internal/identity/sessions_test.go`): fifteen goroutines call
    `ExtendSession` concurrently against one already-stale session and
    sum the rows-affected each call reports, asserting the total is
    exactly 1 — a direct write count from Postgres, not an inference
    from the final value. This calls `ExtendSession` directly rather
    than driving it through `resolveSessionCaller` over HTTP, deliberately:
    going through the middleware reintroduces the exact nondeterminism
    that produced the earlier false-positive ("later requests skip the
    call entirely because they already observed the renewal"), which is
    a legitimate reason for fewer attempts and not what this test exists
    to isolate. Confirmed to fail before the fix — reverting the
    predicate's `- interval '1 minute'` slack locally and re-running this
    test produced totals of 12, 14 and 15 across five runs, never 1 —
    and to pass reliably after it (ten consecutive runs, `-race`), before
    the revert was undone.

---

### Task 11: Login, logout, registration and invite redemption over REST

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/web/api_auth.go`
- Modify: `internal/web/server.go`
- Test: `internal/web/api_auth_test.go`

`handleRegister` calls `identity.CreateUser` with a `CreateUserRequest`
struct now, not four positional arguments (Task 5, Correction 11), and
`writeRegistrationError` gains three more cases — `ErrEmailInvalid`,
`ErrDisplayNameInvalid`, `ErrPasswordInvalid` — mapped to 422, since Task 5
added those typed errors specifically so this HTTP boundary could
distinguish "malformed input" from the other 400/403/409 cases already
handled. Both are reflected in the snippet below.

Task 6's round-2 review (Corrections 8-11) changes this task's shape in
four ways, all reflected in the snippet below:

- `identity.Limiter.Allow` is gone; `handleLogin` calls `Allowed` before
  attempting the login and `Record` only if `Authenticate` fails. Nothing
  calls `Reset` any more — a successful login was never charged in the
  first place, so there is nothing to refund.
- `handleLogin` normalizes `req.Email` (lower-case, trim) before using it
  as the limiter key, matching `Authenticate`'s own normalization, so
  three different capitalizations of the same address share one budget
  instead of three.
- `handleRegister`'s invite-redemption limiter keys on the request's
  client IP (`clientIP(r)`, added below) instead of `req.InviteToken` —
  keying on the secret being guessed let every guess mint a fresh,
  unlimited budget.
- `identity.SessionTTL` no longer exists; `IssueSession` returns
  `(token string, expiresAt time.Time, err error)`, and
  `setSessionCookie` takes `expiresAt` as a parameter instead of
  recomputing `time.Now().Add(identity.SessionTTL)` itself.

**This task does not own sliding session renewal.** `ExtendSession`
(Task 6) named "Tasks 10/11" as its future owner while that was still
undecided; it is settled now, in Task 10, not here. `resolveSessionCaller`
in `internal/web/auth.go` extends a session past halfway through its
`SESSION_TTL` on any authenticated request that carries it — login,
logout and registration below never see the requests that follow a
session past its issuing request, so they are structurally the wrong
place to decide "still active, worth renewing"; the middleware every
subsequent request passes through is the only place that can. Nothing in
this task's handlers should call `ExtendSession`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`internal/web/api_auth_test.go`:

```go
package web_test

import (
	"context"
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/web"
)

func TestLoginSetsSessionCookieAndLogoutClearsIt(t *testing.T) {
	srv, ids, _ := newTestServer(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	if _, err := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "designer@studio.com", DisplayName: "Designer", Password: "password12345"}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("CreateUser: %v", err)
	}

	body := strings.NewReader(`{"email":"designer@studio.com","password":"password12345"}`)
	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "/api/auth/login", body)
	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)

	if rec.Code != http.StatusOK {
		t.Fatalf("login status = %d, want 200; body = %s", rec.Code, rec.Body.String())
	}
	cookies := rec.Result().Cookies()
	var session *http.Cookie
	for _, c := range cookies {
		if c.Name == web.SessionCookie {
			session = c
		}
	}
	if session == nil {
		t.Fatal("login did not set a session cookie")
	}
	if !session.HttpOnly {
		t.Fatal("the session cookie must be HttpOnly")
	}
	if session.SameSite != http.SameSiteLaxMode {
		t.Fatal("the session cookie must be SameSite=Lax")
	}

	meReq := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/api/me", nil)
	meReq.AddCookie(session)
	meRec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(meRec, meReq)
	if meRec.Code != http.StatusOK {
		t.Fatalf("/api/me with the session cookie = %d, want 200", meRec.Code)
	}

	outReq := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "/api/auth/logout", nil)
	outReq.AddCookie(session)
	outRec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(outRec, outReq)
	if outRec.Code != http.StatusNoContent {
		t.Fatalf("logout status = %d, want 204", outRec.Code)
	}

	afterReq := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/api/me", nil)
	afterReq.AddCookie(session)
	afterRec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(afterRec, afterReq)
	if afterRec.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
		t.Fatalf("/api/me after logout = %d, want 401", afterRec.Code)
	}
}

func TestLoginWithWrongPasswordIsUnauthorized(t *testing.T) {
	srv, ids, _ := newTestServer(t)
	if _, err := ids.CreateUser(context.Background(), identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "designer@studio.com", DisplayName: "Designer", Password: "password12345"}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("CreateUser: %v", err)
	}

	body := strings.NewReader(`{"email":"designer@studio.com","password":"nope"}`)
	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "/api/auth/login", body)
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)

	if rec.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
		t.Fatalf("status = %d, want 401", rec.Code)
	}
}

func TestRegisterIsRejectedInInviteOnlyMode(t *testing.T) {
	srv, _, _ := newTestServer(t)
	body := strings.NewReader(`{"email":"new@studio.com","display_name":"New","password":"password12345"}`)
	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "/api/auth/register", body)
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)

	if rec.Code != http.StatusForbidden {
		t.Fatalf("status = %d, want 403 in invite_only mode", rec.Code)
	}
}
```

Run: `go test ./internal/web/ -run TestLogin -v`
Expected: FAIL, 404 instead of 200 (the route does not exist).

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

`internal/web/api_auth.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"strings"
	"time"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/config"
	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/identity"
)

type loginRequest struct {
	Email    string `json:"email"`
	Password string `json:"password"`
}

type registerRequest struct {
	Email       string `json:"email"`
	DisplayName string `json:"display_name"`
	Password    string `json:"password"`
	InviteToken string `json:"invite_token"`
}

func (s *Server) handleLogin(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	var req loginRequest
	if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&req); err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "bad_request", "malformed JSON body")
		return
	}
	// Normalized the same way Authenticate normalizes its own lookup
	// (lower-case, trim), so "Bob@x.com", "bob@x.com" and " bob@x.com "
	// share one rate-limit budget instead of three (Task 6, Correction 9).
	email := strings.ToLower(strings.TrimSpace(req.Email))
	if !s.loginLimiter.Allowed(email) {
		writeError(w, http.StatusTooManyRequests, "rate_limited", "too many attempts, wait a minute")
		return
	}

	user, err := s.opts.Identity.Authenticate(r.Context(), req.Email, req.Password)
	if err != nil {
		s.loginLimiter.Record(email)
		writeError(w, http.StatusUnauthorized, "unauthorized", "email or password is wrong")
		return
	}
	// No Reset call here: Allowed never charges the budget, so a
	// successful login never spent anything that needs refunding (Task 6,
	// Correction 8). The old Allow/Reset shape had an early return between
	// the charge and the refund — exactly the one below, if IssueSession
	// fails — that would have silently spent a legitimate user's budget.

	token, expiresAt, err := s.opts.Identity.IssueSession(r.Context(), user.ID)
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "could not start a session")
		return
	}
	s.setSessionCookie(w, r, token, expiresAt)
	writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, map[string]any{
		"user_id":      user.ID,
		"display_name": user.DisplayName,
		"is_admin":     user.IsAdmin,
	})
}

func (s *Server) handleLogout(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	if cookie, err := r.Cookie(SessionCookie); err == nil {
		_ = s.opts.Identity.RevokeSession(r.Context(), cookie.Value)
	}
	http.SetCookie(w, &http.Cookie{
		Name:     SessionCookie,
		Value:    "",
		Path:     "/",
		MaxAge:   -1,
		HttpOnly: true,
		SameSite: http.SameSiteLaxMode,
	})
	w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
}

func (s *Server) handleRegister(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	var req registerRequest
	if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&req); err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "bad_request", "malformed JSON body")
		return
	}

	// An invite token wins over the instance mode: that is the point of a link.
	if req.InviteToken != "" {
		// Keyed on the client's source IP, not req.InviteToken: the token
		// is the very secret being guessed, so keying the limiter on it
		// would hand every guess a fresh, unlimited budget — the limiter
		// would cap nothing (Task 6, Correction 10).
		ip := clientIP(r)
		if !s.inviteLimiter.Allowed(ip) {
			writeError(w, http.StatusTooManyRequests, "rate_limited", "too many attempts, wait a minute")
			return
		}
		user, err := s.opts.Identity.RedeemInvite(r.Context(), req.InviteToken, req.Email, req.DisplayName, req.Password)
		if err != nil {
			s.inviteLimiter.Record(ip)
			s.writeRegistrationError(w, err)
			return
		}
		s.startSessionFor(w, r, user.ID)
		return
	}

	if s.opts.Config.RegistrationMode != config.RegistrationDomainOpen {
		writeError(w, http.StatusForbidden, "invite_required", "this instance only admits invited users")
		return
	}
	user, err := s.opts.Identity.CreateUser(r.Context(), identity.CreateUserRequest{
		Email:       req.Email,
		DisplayName: req.DisplayName,
		Password:    req.Password,
	})
	if err != nil {
		s.writeRegistrationError(w, err)
		return
	}
	s.startSessionFor(w, r, user.ID)
}

func (s *Server) writeRegistrationError(w http.ResponseWriter, err error) {
	switch {
	case errors.Is(err, identity.ErrInviteInvalid):
		writeError(w, http.StatusForbidden, "invite_invalid", "this invite is not usable")
	case errors.Is(err, identity.ErrEmailTaken):
		writeError(w, http.StatusConflict, "email_taken", "that email already has an account")
	case errors.Is(err, identity.ErrEmailNotAllowed):
		writeError(w, http.StatusForbidden, "email_not_allowed", "that email domain cannot register here")
	case errors.Is(err, identity.ErrEmailInvalid):
		writeError(w, http.StatusUnprocessableEntity, "email_invalid", "that email is not a valid address")
	case errors.Is(err, identity.ErrDisplayNameInvalid):
		writeError(w, http.StatusUnprocessableEntity, "display_name_invalid", "that display name is not valid")
	case errors.Is(err, identity.ErrPasswordInvalid):
		writeError(w, http.StatusUnprocessableEntity, "password_invalid", "that password does not meet requirements")
	default:
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "bad_request", err.Error())
	}
}

func (s *Server) startSessionFor(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, userID uuidValue) {
	token, expiresAt, err := s.opts.Identity.IssueSession(r.Context(), userID)
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "could not start a session")
		return
	}
	s.setSessionCookie(w, r, token, expiresAt)
	writeJSON(w, http.StatusCreated, map[string]any{"user_id": userID})
}

// setSessionCookie takes expiresAt from IssueSession rather than
// recomputing time.Now().Add(...) itself: identity.SessionTTL no longer
// exists as an exported constant (it moved into config.Config, Task 6
// Correction 11), and a second computation of the same policy here could
// silently drift from whatever was actually written to the database.
func (s *Server) setSessionCookie(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, token string, expiresAt time.Time) {
	http.SetCookie(w, &http.Cookie{
		Name:     SessionCookie,
		Value:    token,
		Path:     "/",
		Expires:  expiresAt,
		HttpOnly: true,
		Secure:   r.TLS != nil || r.Header.Get("X-Forwarded-Proto") == "https",
		SameSite: http.SameSiteLaxMode,
	})
}

// clientIP extracts the request's source IP for rate-limiting purposes. It
// deliberately does not consult X-Forwarded-For or similar headers: those
// are attacker-controlled unless a trusted reverse proxy is known to set
// them exactly once, which this instance's deployment shape does not yet
// guarantee, and trusting a spoofable header here would reopen exactly the
// "attacker picks their own key" problem Correction 10 fixed.
func clientIP(r *http.Request) string {
	host, _, err := net.SplitHostPort(r.RemoteAddr)
	if err != nil {
		return r.RemoteAddr
	}
	return host
}
```

**Corrections made during implementation** (this task's own review pass,
reading the plan sceptically per the warning at the top of this task):

1. `writeRegistrationError`'s switch is missing an `ErrInviteExpired`
   case in the plan's own snippet, even though the task's prose and
   `identity.ErrInviteExpired`'s own doc comment (Task 7) both say this
   error exists specifically so a designer clicking a stale invite link
   can be told "this expired" rather than a generic "not valid". Without
   the case, `errors.Is(err, identity.ErrInviteInvalid)` never matches
   (`ErrInviteExpired` does not wrap it — they are separate sentinels),
   so an expired invite would have fallen through to the `default` branch
   below instead. Added a case ahead of `ErrInviteInvalid` mapping it to
   403 `invite_expired`.
2. The plan's `default` branch of `writeRegistrationError` returns
   `http.StatusBadRequest` with `err.Error()` as the message. That default
   is reached for a wrapped, non-sentinel error — e.g. `CreateUser`'s or
   `RedeemInvite`'s own `fmt.Errorf("create user: %w", err)` around a
   genuine database failure — and echoing `err.Error()` back to an
   unauthenticated client risks leaking a raw driver or SQL error message
   (a constraint name, a column, occasionally a fragment of a query).
   Changed the default to a generic 500 `internal_error` with a fixed
   message, matching how `handleLogin`'s own `IssueSession` failure is
   already handled a few lines above it in the same file, instead of
   reusing 400 (which also mischaracterizes a server-side failure as a
   client mistake).
3. **Request body size is bounded**, per this task's own brief ("the
   request body must be bounded here, because the hashing layer
   deliberately does not cap password length"): every handler in
   `api_auth.go` reads its JSON body through a shared `decodeAuthBody`
   helper that wraps `r.Body` in `http.MaxBytesReader` at 16KiB before
   decoding, rather than decoding an unbounded body directly as the
   plan's snippet does. The plan's Step 3 snippet never mentioned this
   despite the task's own prose requiring it; the omission would have
   left `handleLogin`/`handleRegister` as the one place in the request
   path where an attacker-controlled password could still grow
   arbitrarily large before `prepareUser`'s own byte-length check ever
   ran, defeating the point of Task 10 pushing that requirement here.
4. Added `newTestServerWithConfig` to `internal/web/auth_test.go` (not in
   the plan's snippet) so `domain_open`-mode tests can adjust
   `RegistrationMode` without mutating the shared `testConfig()` default
   every other test in the package relies on staying `invite_only`.
5. Test coverage goes beyond the plan's three given tests: added cases
   for a wrong-password login and an unknown-email login returning
   byte-identical bodies (the enumeration question the task brief asks
   about explicitly); the login limiter keying on the *normalized* email
   so case variants share one budget while a different account's budget
   stays untouched; a successful login never spending rate-limit budget
   across repeated logins; invite redemption's limiter keying on source
   IP so ten distinct token guesses from one address share a budget while
   a different address does not; an oversized body being rejected;
   malformed JSON; an expired invite reporting `invite_expired`
   specifically (not just any 403); and the login response never
   containing a password hash. None of these were in the plan's Step 1
   snippet, which only asserted the cookie shape, a wrong password, and
   invite-only mode rejecting registration.
6. **`handleLogin` was keyed only on the normalized email, with no
   second key.** That leaves a targeted-lockout gap the plan's own
   snippet never named: anyone who knows a colleague's address — not
   hard, in a small studio — can spend that account's 10/min budget
   indefinitely, from anywhere, at no cost to themselves, simply by
   submitting wrong passwords for it. `handleLogin` now checks two
   *independent* budgets before ever calling `Authenticate` —
   `s.loginLimiter.Allowed(email) && s.loginIPLimiter.Allowed(ip)` — and
   records both on a failed attempt. `loginIPLimiter` uses a looser cap
   (40/min) than `loginLimiter` (10/min), since one source IP
   legitimately carries more traffic than one person (a shared office
   NAT).

   The two budgets are deliberately **not** combined into a single
   composite `email+ip` key — this is the part most likely to look like
   a harmless simplification later, so it is called out explicitly both
   here and in `Server`'s own field comment in `server.go`: a composite
   key would hand an attacker who rotates source IPs a fresh, unlimited
   per-account budget on every hop, which defeats the entire point of
   keying on email in the first place. Keeping them as two separate
   `*identity.Limiter` instances, both consulted and both recorded, is
   what lets each one keep its own distinct property — one caps
   per-account abuse regardless of origin, the other caps per-origin
   abuse regardless of which accounts it is spread across — without
   either one's cap becoming trivially bypassable via the other axis.

   Both checks run, and both fail the same way (429, before
   `Authenticate` is ever invoked), whichever budget is actually
   exhausted: a response that revealed *which* limiter tripped, or that
   let a correct password through once a budget ran dry, would reopen
   the exact enumeration oracle `Authenticate`'s sentinel hash exists to
   close — a status code or its timing would leak whether the password
   was right independently of the rate limit. A self-inflicted lockout
   (own email budget exhausted) recovers on its own once the window
   rolls forward; an oracle does not, which is why "honour a correct
   password past the limit" was rejected as a fix even though it is the
   tempting one.

   Invite redemption's `inviteLimiter` stays IP-only, deliberately not
   given the same email+IP pairing: the attacker model differs. There is
   no *account* for req.Email to lock out here — reaching `RedeemInvite`
   at all already requires holding the actual invite token, which is the
   credential under attack, and an invite bound to an email the caller
   doesn't hold is simply rejected by `RedeemInvite`'s own comparison
   without costing the real invitee anything. Adding a second key keyed
   on `req.Email` here would protect against a threat that doesn't
   exist at this endpoint, for a field that isn't even always present
   (an unbound invite has no email at all).

**Corrections made during implementation, round 2** (a second quality
review, requested against commit `7b23356`; the first round of
corrections above is unchanged and still in effect — this round found
defects the first review pass did not, mostly sharing one root cause the
reviewer named directly: the code reasoned carefully about the adversary
and not at all about the deployment):

1. **`clientIP` was correct about spoofing and wrong about production.**
   It read only `r.RemoteAddr` and refused `X-Forwarded-For`
   unconditionally — sound reasoning for a directly exposed instance, but
   Maestro is commonly deployed behind a reverse proxy, and there
   `RemoteAddr` is the proxy's own address on *every* request. Left at
   its old behavior in that deployment, the per-IP invite (and, after
   this round, register and login) limiter collapses into one global
   10-or-40-per-minute bucket shared by the whole instance: any
   unauthenticated caller spends it in ten requests and every invite
   redemption — a denial of onboarding — or every login from anyone else
   returns 429 until the window rolls. Fixed with a new
   `config.Config.TrustedProxyCount` (env `TRUSTED_PROXY_COUNT`,
   default 0 — a directly exposed instance, identical behavior to
   before) and a `clientIP` that only reads `X-Forwarded-For` when it is
   positive, trusting exactly that many rightmost entries as appended by
   that many trusted hops. See `TrustedProxyCount`'s own doc comment in
   `internal/config/config.go` and `clientIP`'s in
   `internal/web/api_auth.go` for the full algorithm and failure modes
   in both directions (too low collapses the bucket; too high lets a
   direct caller forge the trusted entry).
2. **`domain_open` self-service registration had no rate limit at all.**
   The limiter lived inside the invite branch of `handleRegister` only,
   so the self-service branch reached `identity.CreateUser` — a full
   argon2 derivation — at unlimited rate, and (since this product sends
   no verification email, so the usual mitigation is unavailable) was an
   unauthenticated, unlimited-rate enumeration oracle on two axes: the
   response distinguishes an out-of-domain address from a malformed one
   from a taken one from success, and 409-vs-201 enumerates which
   accounts exist — undoing the protection `handleLogin` provides, since
   an attacker who cannot tell a bad password from an unknown email at
   `/api/auth/login` can just ask `/api/auth/register` instead. Fixed by
   moving the IP check to the top of `handleRegister`, before either
   branch, and renaming `inviteLimiter` to `registerIPLimiter` to reflect
   that it now guards the endpoint as a whole (10/min, shared across both
   branches — see `Server`'s own field comment in `server.go` for why a
   single shared budget, not two, is intentional here). This depended on
   item 1 above: an IP-keyed limiter is meaningless until `clientIP`
   can actually see the real client behind a proxy.
3. **Logout reported success when revocation failed.** The error was
   discarded with `_ =`, so a transient database failure cleared the
   cookie, returned 204, and left the session valid server-side — anyone
   else holding a copy of that cookie kept access while the user had just
   been told they were logged out. `handleLogout` now logs the failure
   with `slog.ErrorContext` and returns 500 without clearing the cookie,
   so the client can retry and the cookie is only cleared once revocation
   is confirmed.
4. **No 500 path logged anything server-side.** Correction 2 (round 1)
   correctly stopped echoing `err.Error()` to the client on
   `writeRegistrationError`'s default branch, but the underlying error
   then went nowhere — an operator had no trace of a database fault.
   Every 500 path in `api_auth.go` (`handleLogin`'s and
   `startSessionFor`'s `IssueSession` failures, `writeRegistrationError`'s
   default branch, and the new logout failure in item 3) now logs with
   `slog.ErrorContext` before writing the generic client message.
5. **An invite did not actually beat the domain allowlist.** Fixed in
   `internal/identity` — see Task 7's Corrections 14 and 15 above for the
   full explanation; it is recorded there because it changes `invites.go`
   and `users.go`, not this task's own files. **This item was itself
   corrected in a third review pass:** the fix that shipped in this round
   had `RedeemInvite` skip `ALLOWED_EMAIL_DOMAINS` for every redemption,
   which was broader than intended — an *unbound* invite names no domain
   at all, so the allowlist is still the only policy anyone has stated
   for it, and skipping it there let any unbound invite's holder register
   with any address whatsoever. Task 7's Correction 15 narrows this to a
   per-invite choice: skip the check for a *bound* invite (the admin
   named this exact address, and `CreateInvite` already applied the
   policy to it at creation time), keep it for an *unbound* one. The
   framing above ("holding the invite token is the authorization") was
   only ever true of a bound invite; an unbound one authorizes nothing
   about *which* address may use it, which is the distinction
   Correction 15 encodes in code.
6. **`setSessionCookie` trusted `X-Forwarded-Proto` unconditionally while
   `clientIP` refused the equivalent header for `X-Forwarded-For`.**
   Inconsistent, and dangerous in the omission direction: behind a
   TLS-terminating proxy that does not set the header, `r.TLS` is nil and
   the session cookie would ship without `Secure` on an HTTPS site.
   Folded into the same `TrustedProxyCount` decision as item 1: a new
   shared `sessionCookieTemplate` (used by both `setSessionCookie` and
   the new `clearSessionCookie`, folding in the minor "route logout's
   cookie through the same helper" fix below) only treats
   `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` as HTTPS when `s.behindTrustedProxy()`.
7. **The `domain_open` tests ran against a configuration `config.Load`
   itself rejects.** They left `AllowedEmailDomains` empty, which
   `Load` refuses for `domain_open`, and — independently of that —
   `Config.EmailAllowed` returns true unconditionally for an empty list,
   so the domain gate was silently disabled in every one of those tests
   and `email_not_allowed` was never exercised at the HTTP layer at all.
   `internal/web/api_auth_test.go` now has a `domainOpenConfig` helper
   that sets a real `AllowedEmailDomains` alongside the mode, and a new
   `TestRegisterWithOffDomainEmailInDomainOpenModeIsForbidden` exercises
   the rejected-domain case the old tests never reached.
8. **Test gaps.** Added: `TestDoubleRedemptionOverHTTPFailsTheSecondTime`
   (an invite link clicked twice over the actual HTTP path, not just at
   the identity layer — named by the reviewer as the single most likely
   real-world failure); `TestRegisterWithInviteTokenWinsOverDomainOpenMode`
   (invite plus `domain_open` together); `TestRegisterWithOffDomainBoundInviteSucceeds`
   and `TestRegisterWithOffDomainUnboundInviteIsForbidden`, and at the
   identity layer `TestRedeemBoundInviteAllowsOffDomainEmail` and
   `TestRedeemUnboundInviteAppliesDomainAllowlist` (item 5, further
   narrowed by the third review pass — see Task 7's Correction 15);
   `TestRegisterWithEmptyDisplayNameIsUnprocessable` and
   `TestRegisterWithShortPasswordIsUnprocessable` (the
   `display_name_invalid`/`password_invalid` branches of
   `writeRegistrationError`, previously untested at the HTTP layer);
   `TestClientIPIgnoresXForwardedForByDefault` and
   `TestClientIPHonorsXForwardedForBehindTrustedProxy` (item 1);
   `TestSessionCookieNotSecureOverForwardedProtoWithoutTrustedProxy` and
   `TestSessionCookieSecureOverForwardedProtoBehindTrustedProxy` (item 6);
   and `TestRegisterInDomainOpenModeIsRateLimitedByIP` (item 2).

   Minors also applied: `handleLogout`'s doc comment now states that
   revocation is checked only at request admission and does not tear
   down an already-open connection, pointing at Task 14's SSE stream as
   the place that limitation actually matters; an oversized body now
   returns 413 (`http.MaxBytesError`, via `errors.As`) instead of a
   generic 400, and `TestOversizedRegisterBodyIsRejectedWith413` was
   updated to match; every handler in this file now requires a
   `Content-Type: application/json` request (415 otherwise —
   `TestLoginRejectsNonJSONContentType`,
   `TestLoginRejectsMissingContentType`); an empty email is rejected in
   `handleLogin` before either rate limiter is touched
   (`TestLoginWithEmptyEmailIsBadRequest`); and `setSessionCookie` and
   the new `clearSessionCookie` both build their `*http.Cookie` from a
   shared `sessionCookieTemplate` so Path, HttpOnly, Secure and SameSite
   cannot drift between the "set" and "clear" call sites.

### Task 12: Project and token REST endpoints, with single-game navigation

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/web/api_projects.go`, `internal/web/api_tokens.go`
- Modify: `internal/web/server.go`
- Test: `internal/web/api_projects_test.go`

`api_projects.go` also carries membership management
(`handleListMembers`, `handleChangeRole`, `handleRemoveMember`): listing,
promoting/demoting and removing are project-scoped exactly like the game
and token endpoints already in this file, and this task is the first and
only place in the plan authorized to decide who may call
`projects.SetRole`/`RemoveMember`/`ListMembers` — see Task 8's
corrections for why those methods exist with no caller until here.

**This task owns the authorization companion to Task 10's Caller.**
Task 10 deliberately stopped at authentication (`web.Caller`, `IsToken()`,
`ScopedProject()`) and did not build `requireProjectMember` or any
equivalent — its own plan corrections say why: that check needs the
endpoint shapes this task defines (which routes carry a `{game}` path
segment, which are token-only, which need a specific role) to be real,
not guessed at from a task with no REST surface yet. Build it here, on
top of `Caller.ScopedProject()` and `Caller.IsToken()` rather than
`Caller`'s raw `TokenID`/`ProjectID` fields: `ScopedProject()` is where
Task 10 already encoded "an admin is not exempt from a token's binding",
so a `requireProjectMember` built on the raw fields instead would have
to re-derive that itself, with a real chance of re-deriving it wrong for
one route out of the six or so this task adds.

**Removing a member already revokes their tokens in that game** —
`projects.RemoveMember` does this itself, inside its own transaction,
as of Task 9 (its Round 2 Correction 9). An earlier draft of this
section routed that fix here, to `handleRemoveMember`; a later review
correctly rejected that, since Task 10's auth middleware starts trusting
a resolved token's project binding as its scope several tasks before
this one would have shipped. Nothing in this task needs to do anything
further for it: `handleRemoveMember` calling `projects.RemoveMember`
already gets the revocation for free, and must not also call
`identity.RevokeAPIToken` itself — a second, unrelated call here would
only reintroduce the crash window Task 9 closed by putting the
revocation inside `RemoveMember`'s own transaction.

Also noted by Task 8's Round 2 corrections, as a follow-on to the
membership endpoints below rather than something to build now: with
Task 8's last-owner guard in place and no way to delete a project
anywhere in this plan, a designer who creates a game alone has no way to
ever leave or delete it — the only exit today is an operator in `psql`.
When this task (or a later one) adds `DELETE /api/games/{game}`, it is
owner-only, like `handleChangeRole` and unlike self-removal from
`handleRemoveMember`, and it maps `projects.ErrLastOwner` the same
`409 last_owner` way the membership endpoints below already do — though
in practice a project's own delete can never actually hit that guard: a
project always has at least one owner by construction (`Create` grants
one atomically, and Task 8's guard refuses to let the last one be
demoted or removed), so `DELETE /api/games/{game}` never needs to worry
about deleting an ownerless project, only about who is allowed to ask for
one to be deleted at all. This endpoint is not implemented in this task;
it is named here so the gap has an owner instead of being rediscovered.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`internal/web/api_projects_test.go`:

```go
package web_test

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/web"
)

// loginAs creates a user, logs in, and returns the session cookie.
func loginAs(t *testing.T, srv *web.Server, email string) *http.Cookie {
	t.Helper()
	body := strings.NewReader(`{"email":"` + email + `","password":"password12345"}`)
	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "/api/auth/login", body)
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)
	if rec.Code != http.StatusOK {
		t.Fatalf("login for %s = %d: %s", email, rec.Code, rec.Body.String())
	}
	for _, c := range rec.Result().Cookies() {
		if c.Name == web.SessionCookie {
			return c
		}
	}
	t.Fatalf("no session cookie for %s", email)
	return nil
}

func TestListGamesOnlyShowsMemberships(t *testing.T) {
	srv, ids, projSvc := newTestServer(t)
	ctx := context.Background()

	insider, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "in@studio.com", DisplayName: "In", Password: "password12345"})
	if _, err := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "out@studio.com", DisplayName: "Out", Password: "password12345"}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("CreateUser: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := projSvc.Create(ctx, "azeroth", "Azeroth", insider.ID); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Create: %v", err)
	}

	for email, want := range map[string]int{"in@studio.com": 1, "out@studio.com": 0} {
		cookie := loginAs(t, srv, email)
		req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/api/games", nil)
		req.AddCookie(cookie)
		rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
		srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)

		var body struct {
			Games []struct {
				Slug string `json:"slug"`
			} `json:"games"`
		}
		if err := json.NewDecoder(rec.Body).Decode(&body); err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("decode for %s: %v", email, err)
		}
		if len(body.Games) != want {
			t.Fatalf("%s sees %d games, want %d", email, len(body.Games), want)
		}
	}
}

func TestRootRedirectsToTheOnlyGame(t *testing.T) {
	srv, ids, projSvc := newTestServer(t)
	ctx := context.Background()

	user, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "solo@studio.com", DisplayName: "Solo", Password: "password12345"})
	if _, err := projSvc.Create(ctx, "azeroth", "Azeroth", user.ID); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Create: %v", err)
	}
	cookie := loginAs(t, srv, "solo@studio.com")

	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/", nil)
	req.AddCookie(cookie)
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)

	if rec.Code != http.StatusFound {
		t.Fatalf("status = %d, want 302", rec.Code)
	}
	if got := rec.Header().Get("Location"); got != "/g/azeroth" {
		t.Fatalf("Location = %q, want /g/azeroth", got)
	}
}

func TestRootShowsPickerWithTwoGames(t *testing.T) {
	srv, ids, projSvc := newTestServer(t)
	ctx := context.Background()

	user, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "two@studio.com", DisplayName: "Two", Password: "password12345"})
	if _, err := projSvc.Create(ctx, "azeroth", "Azeroth", user.ID); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Create: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := projSvc.Create(ctx, "le-mans", "Le Mans", user.ID); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Create: %v", err)
	}
	cookie := loginAs(t, srv, "two@studio.com")

	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/", nil)
	req.AddCookie(cookie)
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)

	if rec.Code != http.StatusOK {
		t.Fatalf("status = %d, want 200 (the picker)", rec.Code)
	}
}

func TestTokenCreationRequiresMembership(t *testing.T) {
	srv, ids, projSvc := newTestServer(t)
	ctx := context.Background()

	owner, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "owner@studio.com", DisplayName: "Owner", Password: "password12345"})
	if _, err := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "stranger@studio.com", DisplayName: "Stranger", Password: "password12345"}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("CreateUser: %v", err)
	}
	project, _ := projSvc.Create(ctx, "azeroth", "Azeroth", owner.ID)

	cookie := loginAs(t, srv, "stranger@studio.com")
	body := strings.NewReader(`{"label":"sneaky"}`)
	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "/api/games/"+project.ID.String()+"/tokens", body)
	req.AddCookie(cookie)
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)

	if rec.Code != http.StatusForbidden {
		t.Fatalf("status = %d, want 403", rec.Code)
	}
}

func TestTokenIsReturnedOnceOnCreation(t *testing.T) {
	srv, ids, projSvc := newTestServer(t)
	ctx := context.Background()

	owner, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "owner@studio.com", DisplayName: "Owner", Password: "password12345"})
	project, _ := projSvc.Create(ctx, "azeroth", "Azeroth", owner.ID)
	cookie := loginAs(t, srv, "owner@studio.com")

	body := strings.NewReader(`{"label":"seed agent"}`)
	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "/api/games/"+project.ID.String()+"/tokens", body)
	req.AddCookie(cookie)
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)

	if rec.Code != http.StatusCreated {
		t.Fatalf("status = %d, want 201: %s", rec.Code, rec.Body.String())
	}
	var created struct {
		Token string `json:"token"`
	}
	if err := json.NewDecoder(rec.Body).Decode(&created); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("decode: %v", err)
	}
	if !strings.HasPrefix(created.Token, "mst_") {
		t.Fatalf("token = %q, want the mst_ prefix", created.Token)
	}

	listReq := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/api/games/"+project.ID.String()+"/tokens", nil)
	listReq.AddCookie(cookie)
	listRec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(listRec, listReq)
	if strings.Contains(listRec.Body.String(), created.Token) {
		t.Fatal("the listing leaked the clear token value")
	}
}

func TestListMembersRequiresMembership(t *testing.T) {
	srv, ids, projSvc := newTestServer(t)
	ctx := context.Background()

	owner, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "owner@studio.com", DisplayName: "Owner", Password: "password12345"})
	if _, err := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "stranger@studio.com", DisplayName: "Stranger", Password: "password12345"}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("CreateUser: %v", err)
	}
	project, _ := projSvc.Create(ctx, "azeroth", "Azeroth", owner.ID)

	cookie := loginAs(t, srv, "stranger@studio.com")
	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/api/games/"+project.ID.String()+"/members", nil)
	req.AddCookie(cookie)
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)

	if rec.Code != http.StatusForbidden {
		t.Fatalf("status = %d, want 403", rec.Code)
	}
}

func TestOnlyOwnerCanChangeRole(t *testing.T) {
	srv, ids, projSvc := newTestServer(t)
	ctx := context.Background()

	owner, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "owner@studio.com", DisplayName: "Owner", Password: "password12345"})
	editor, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "editor@studio.com", DisplayName: "Editor", Password: "password12345"})
	other, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "other@studio.com", DisplayName: "Other", Password: "password12345"})
	project, _ := projSvc.Create(ctx, "azeroth", "Azeroth", owner.ID)
	if err := projSvc.SetRole(ctx, editor.ID, project.ID, "editor"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("SetRole: %v", err)
	}

	// An editor may not promote or demote anyone, including themselves.
	editorCookie := loginAs(t, srv, "editor@studio.com")
	body := strings.NewReader(`{"role":"owner"}`)
	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPatch, "/api/games/"+project.ID.String()+"/members/"+other.ID.String(), body)
	req.AddCookie(editorCookie)
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)
	if rec.Code != http.StatusForbidden {
		t.Fatalf("editor changing a role: status = %d, want 403", rec.Code)
	}

	// The owner may.
	ownerCookie := loginAs(t, srv, "owner@studio.com")
	body = strings.NewReader(`{"role":"viewer"}`)
	req = httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPatch, "/api/games/"+project.ID.String()+"/members/"+editor.ID.String(), body)
	req.AddCookie(ownerCookie)
	rec = httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)
	if rec.Code != http.StatusOK {
		t.Fatalf("owner changing a role: status = %d, want 200: %s", rec.Code, rec.Body.String())
	}
}

func TestMemberCanRemoveSelfButNotSoleOwner(t *testing.T) {
	srv, ids, projSvc := newTestServer(t)
	ctx := context.Background()

	owner, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "owner@studio.com", DisplayName: "Owner", Password: "password12345"})
	viewer, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "viewer@studio.com", DisplayName: "Viewer", Password: "password12345"})
	project, _ := projSvc.Create(ctx, "azeroth", "Azeroth", owner.ID)
	if err := projSvc.SetRole(ctx, viewer.ID, project.ID, "viewer"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("SetRole: %v", err)
	}

	// A non-owner member may remove themselves.
	viewerCookie := loginAs(t, srv, "viewer@studio.com")
	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodDelete, "/api/games/"+project.ID.String()+"/members/"+viewer.ID.String(), nil)
	req.AddCookie(viewerCookie)
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)
	if rec.Code != http.StatusNoContent {
		t.Fatalf("viewer removing self: status = %d, want 204", rec.Code)
	}

	// The sole remaining owner may not remove themselves.
	ownerCookie := loginAs(t, srv, "owner@studio.com")
	req = httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodDelete, "/api/games/"+project.ID.String()+"/members/"+owner.ID.String(), nil)
	req.AddCookie(ownerCookie)
	rec = httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)
	if rec.Code != http.StatusConflict {
		t.Fatalf("sole owner removing self: status = %d, want 409", rec.Code)
	}
	var body struct {
		Error string `json:"error"`
	}
	if err := json.NewDecoder(rec.Body).Decode(&body); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("decode: %v", err)
	}
	if body.Error != "last_owner" {
		t.Fatalf("error = %q, want last_owner", body.Error)
	}
}

func TestTokenCallerCannotManageMembers(t *testing.T) {
	srv, ids, projSvc := newTestServer(t)
	ctx := context.Background()

	owner, _ := ids.CreateUser(ctx, identity.CreateUserRequest{Email: "owner@studio.com", DisplayName: "Owner", Password: "password12345"})
	project, _ := projSvc.Create(ctx, "azeroth", "Azeroth", owner.ID)
	token, _, err := ids.CreateAPIToken(ctx, identity.CreateAPITokenRequest{ProjectID: project.ID, UserID: owner.ID, Label: "agent"})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("CreateAPIToken: %v", err)
	}

	req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, "/api/games/"+project.ID.String()+"/members", nil)
	req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+token)
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	srv.ServeHTTP(rec, req)

	if rec.Code != http.StatusForbidden {
		t.Fatalf("status = %d, want 403", rec.Code)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the project endpoints**

`internal/web/api_projects.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"net/http"

	"github.com/google/uuid"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/projects"
)

type createGameRequest struct {
	Slug string `json:"slug"`
	Name string `json:"name"`
}

func (s *Server) handleListGames(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, caller Caller) {
	rows, err := s.opts.Projects.ListForUser(r.Context(), caller.UserID)
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "could not list games")
		return
	}
	games := make([]map[string]any, 0, len(rows))
	for _, p := range rows {
		games = append(games, map[string]any{"id": p.ID, "slug": p.Slug, "name": p.Name})
	}
	writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, map[string]any{"games": games})
}

func (s *Server) handleCreateGame(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, caller Caller) {
	var req createGameRequest
	if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&req); err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "bad_request", "malformed JSON body")
		return
	}
	project, err := s.opts.Projects.Create(r.Context(), req.Slug, req.Name, caller.UserID)
	if errors.Is(err, projects.ErrSlugTaken) {
		writeError(w, http.StatusConflict, "slug_taken", "another game already uses that slug")
		return
	}
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "could not create the game")
		return
	}
	writeJSON(w, http.StatusCreated, map[string]any{"id": project.ID, "slug": project.Slug, "name": project.Name})
}

// requireProjectAccess resolves the {game} path value and checks the caller may
// touch it. A token caller may only touch the project its token is bound to,
// admin or not; a session caller must hold a membership.
func (s *Server) requireProjectAccess(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, caller Caller) (uuid.UUID, bool) {
	projectID, err := uuid.Parse(r.PathValue("game"))
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusNotFound, "not_found", "no such game")
		return uuid.Nil, false
	}

	if caller.ProjectID != nil {
		if *caller.ProjectID != projectID {
			writeError(w, http.StatusForbidden, "scope_violation", "this token is bound to another game")
			return uuid.Nil, false
		}
		return projectID, true
	}

	if _, err := s.opts.Projects.RoleOf(r.Context(), caller.UserID, projectID); err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusForbidden, "forbidden", "you are not a member of this game")
		return uuid.Nil, false
	}
	return projectID, true
}

// handleRoot implements the single-game shortcut: one visible game goes
// straight to it, several show the picker.
func (s *Server) handleRoot(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	caller, ok := CallerFrom(r.Context())
	if !ok {
		http.Redirect(w, r, "/login", http.StatusFound)
		return
	}
	games, err := s.opts.Projects.ListForUser(r.Context(), caller.UserID)
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "could not list games")
		return
	}
	if len(games) == 1 {
		http.Redirect(w, r, "/g/"+games[0].Slug, http.StatusFound)
		return
	}
	s.serveAsset(w, r, "index.html")
}

// Membership management: list, promote/demote, remove. All three are
// human-only — a bearer token authenticates an agent scoped to this game's
// content (Task 9's "one token = one project"), not a person entitled to
// decide who else may manage the game, so caller.TokenID != nil is
// rejected here before requireProjectAccess even runs. This is stricter
// than requireProjectAccess's own token-vs-project check: that one lets a
// token touch its own game's content, but membership is never "this
// game's content" for a token caller, only for a human one.
func (s *Server) requireHumanCaller(w http.ResponseWriter, caller Caller) bool {
	if caller.TokenID != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusForbidden, "forbidden", "API tokens cannot manage game membership")
		return false
	}
	return true
}

func (s *Server) handleListMembers(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, caller Caller) {
	if !s.requireHumanCaller(w, caller) {
		return
	}
	projectID, ok := s.requireProjectAccess(w, r, caller)
	if !ok {
		return
	}
	rows, err := s.opts.Projects.ListMembers(r.Context(), projectID)
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "could not list members")
		return
	}
	// No email: projects.Member carries none (Task 8's Round 2
	// corrections), because this endpoint has no owner-only gate of its
	// own — any member, viewer included, can list members — and a
	// teammate's email is not something every role should be able to
	// read.
	members := make([]map[string]any, 0, len(rows))
	for _, m := range rows {
		members = append(members, map[string]any{
			"id": m.UserID, "display_name": m.DisplayName, "role": m.Role,
		})
	}
	writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, map[string]any{"members": members})
}

type changeRoleRequest struct {
	Role string `json:"role"`
}

// handleChangeRole promotes or demotes an existing member, or grants
// membership to a user who has none yet — projects.SetRole upserts, and an
// owner directly granting a role without a round trip through an invite
// link is a legitimate shortcut, not a bug, precisely because only an
// owner may reach this handler at all. Only an owner may change any
// role, including their own: unlike removal (handleRemoveMember below),
// there is no self-service path here, so a non-owner can never promote
// themselves.
func (s *Server) handleChangeRole(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, caller Caller) {
	if !s.requireHumanCaller(w, caller) {
		return
	}
	projectID, ok := s.requireProjectAccess(w, r, caller)
	if !ok {
		return
	}
	if role, err := s.opts.Projects.RoleOf(r.Context(), caller.UserID, projectID); err != nil || role != "owner" {
		writeError(w, http.StatusForbidden, "forbidden", "only an owner may change a member's role")
		return
	}
	targetID, err := uuid.Parse(r.PathValue("user"))
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusNotFound, "not_found", "no such member")
		return
	}
	var req changeRoleRequest
	if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&req); err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "bad_request", "malformed JSON body")
		return
	}

	err = s.opts.Projects.SetRole(r.Context(), targetID, projectID, req.Role)
	switch {
	case errors.Is(err, projects.ErrRoleInvalid):
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "invalid_role", "role must be one of owner, editor, viewer")
	case errors.Is(err, projects.ErrLastOwner):
		// See handleRemoveMember's doc comment: this is the same guard,
		// reached here when the change would demote the game's only owner.
		writeError(w, http.StatusConflict, "last_owner", "a game must keep at least one owner — promote someone else first")
	case err != nil:
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "could not change the member's role")
	default:
		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
	}
}

// handleRemoveMember removes a member from a game. An owner may remove any
// member; anyone may remove themselves (leaving a game they are a member
// of is always a member's own choice, not a privilege). Either path can
// still hit projects.ErrLastOwner — a sole owner leaving, or an owner
// trying to remove another owner down to zero — which is reported as 409
// so a human sees "promote someone else first" instead of a generic
// failure, and every other row scoped to this game is never left with
// nobody able to manage it. projects.RemoveMember itself (Task 9, Round
// 2 Correction 9) already revokes, in the same transaction as the
// membership deletion, every API token in this game the removed member
// created — this handler does not need to call identity.RevokeAPIToken
// separately, and must not: doing it here as a second, unrelated call
// would reintroduce the crash window Task 9 closed by putting the
// revocation inside RemoveMember's own transaction.
func (s *Server) handleRemoveMember(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, caller Caller) {
	if !s.requireHumanCaller(w, caller) {
		return
	}
	projectID, ok := s.requireProjectAccess(w, r, caller)
	if !ok {
		return
	}
	targetID, err := uuid.Parse(r.PathValue("user"))
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusNotFound, "not_found", "no such member")
		return
	}
	if targetID != caller.UserID {
		if role, rerr := s.opts.Projects.RoleOf(r.Context(), caller.UserID, projectID); rerr != nil || role != "owner" {
			writeError(w, http.StatusForbidden, "forbidden", "only an owner may remove another member")
			return
		}
	}

	err = s.opts.Projects.RemoveMember(r.Context(), targetID, projectID)
	switch {
	case errors.Is(err, projects.ErrLastOwner):
		writeError(w, http.StatusConflict, "last_owner", "a game must keep at least one owner — promote someone else first")
	case err != nil:
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "could not remove the member")
	default:
		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the token endpoints**

`internal/web/api_tokens.go`:

```go
package web

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"net/http"

	"github.com/google/uuid"

	"github.com/neverbot/maestro/internal/identity"
)

type createTokenRequest struct {
	Label string `json:"label"`
}

func (s *Server) handleCreateToken(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, caller Caller) {
	projectID, ok := s.requireProjectAccess(w, r, caller)
	if !ok {
		return
	}
	var req createTokenRequest
	if err := json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&req); err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "bad_request", "malformed JSON body")
		return
	}
	if req.Label == "" {
		req.Label = "unnamed token"
	}

	clear, row, err := s.opts.Identity.CreateAPIToken(r.Context(), identity.CreateAPITokenRequest{ProjectID: projectID, UserID: caller.UserID, Label: req.Label})
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "could not create the token")
		return
	}
	// The clear value appears here and nowhere else, ever.
	writeJSON(w, http.StatusCreated, map[string]any{
		"id":    row.ID,
		"label": row.Label,
		"token": clear,
	})
}

func (s *Server) handleListTokens(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, caller Caller) {
	projectID, ok := s.requireProjectAccess(w, r, caller)
	if !ok {
		return
	}
	rows, err := s.opts.Identity.ListAPITokens(r.Context(), projectID)
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "could not list tokens")
		return
	}
	writeJSON(w, http.StatusOK, map[string]any{"tokens": rows})
}

func (s *Server) handleRevokeToken(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, caller Caller) {
	projectID, ok := s.requireProjectAccess(w, r, caller)
	if !ok {
		return
	}
	tokenID, err := uuid.Parse(r.PathValue("token"))
	if err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusNotFound, "not_found", "no such token")
		return
	}
	if err := s.opts.Identity.RevokeAPIToken(r.Context(), identity.RevokeAPITokenRequest{ProjectID: projectID, TokenID: tokenID}); err != nil {
		writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "could not revoke the token")
		return
	}
	w.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)
}
```

```go
// serveAsset writes an embedded static file. Task 14 replaces this with the
// real embedded filesystem.
func (s *Server) serveAsset(w http.ResponseWriter, _ *http.Request, name string) {
	w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "text/html; charset=utf-8")
	_, _ = w.Write([]byte("<!doctype html><title>Maestro</title><div id=app data-asset=\"" + name + "\"></div>"))
}
```
